[[UFC Fight Night: Hunt vs. Bigfoot]]\n}}\n'''''The Ultimate Fighter: Team Rousey vs. Team Tate Finale''''' (also known as '''''The Ultimate Fighter 18 Finale''''') was a [[mixed martial arts]] event held by the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]].{{cite web |url=http://www.ufc.com/event/TUF18-Finale|title=The Ultimate Fighter 18 Finale|publisher=Ultimate Fighting Championship|access-date=2013-09-09}} It took place on November 30, 2013, at the [[Mandalay Bay Events Center]] in [[Las Vegas Valley|Las Vegas]], [[Nevada]].{{cite web |url=http://www.mmajunkie.com/news/2013/08/the-ultimate-fighter-18-finale-targeted-for-nov-30-at-las-vegas-mandalay-bay|title=The Ultimate Fighter 18 Finale targeted for Nov. 30 at Las Vegas' Mandalay Bay|work=mmajunkie.com |date=August 8, 2013 |access-date=August 23, 2013}}\n\n===Background===\nThe event was expected to be headlined by a [[UFC Flyweight Championship]] bout between the current champion [[Demetrious Johnson]] and top contender [[Joseph Benavidez]].{{cite web |url=http://www.bloodyelbow.com/2013/9/18/4746338/mma-news-demetrious-johnson-vs-joseph-benavidez-tuf-18-finale|title=Demetrious Johnson vs. Joseph Benavidez rematch to headline The Ultimate Fighter 18 Finale|work=bloodyelbow.com |author=Mookie Alexander|date=September 18, 2013 |access-date=September 18, 2013}} The two previously met at [[UFC 152]] in the finals of the inaugural [[List of UFC champions#Tournament winners|Flyweight Tournament]], with Johnson winning the bout via split decision. However, on November 10, it was announced that [[Anthony Pettis]] withdrew from the main event on [[UFC on Fox 9]] and the Johnson/Benavidez bout was moved to that event.{{cite web|url=http://www.mmajunkie.com/news/2013/11/pettis-thomson-off-ufc-on-fox-9-johnson-benavidez-shifts-to-main-event|title=Pettis-Thomson off UFC on FOX 9, Johnson-Benavidez shifts to main event|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|date=2013-11-10|access-date=2013-11-10}}\n\nFeatured on the card were the finals from ''The Ultimate Fighter: Team Rousey vs. Team Tate'' in both men's and women's bantamweight divisions.\n\n[[Zak Cummings]] was originally scheduled to face [[Sérgio Moraes]], but withdrew due to injury. He was replaced by [[Sean Spencer (fighter)|Sean Spencer]], but Moraes would also pull out and [[Drew Dober]] would replace him.{{cite web|url=http://www.mmajunkie.com/news/2013/10/sean-spencer-replaces-injured-zak-cummings-meets-sergio-moraes-at-tuf-18-finale|title=Sean Spencer replaces injured Zak Cummings, meets Sergio Moraes at TUF 18 Finale |publisher=mmajunkie.com |author=Staff|date=2013-10-30|access-date=2013-10-30}}\n\n[[Josh Sampo]] missed the 126 lb weight limit at the weigh ins and forfeited 10% of his purse as a penalty.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2013/11/the-ultimate-fighter-18-finale-live-and-official-weigh-in-results/|title=The Ultimate Fighter 18 Finale weigh-in results: Diaz, Maynard, 'TUF' finalists all cleared|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|date=2013-11-29|access-date=2013-11-29}}\n\n===Results===\n{{MMAevent}}\n{{MMAevent card|Main card (Fox Sports 1)}}\n{{MMAevent Johnson vs. Benavidez 2]]\n}}\n'''''UFC Fight Night: Hunt vs. Bigfoot''''' (also known as '''''UFC Fight Night 33''''') was a [[mixed martial arts]] event held on December 7, 2013, at the [[Brisbane Entertainment Centre]] in [[Brisbane, Queensland|Brisbane]], Australia.{{cite web|url=http://www.ufc.com/news/ufc-brisbane-hunt-vs-bigfoot-press-release-090913|title=Hunt vs. Bigfoot Headlines UFC in Brisbane|publisher=ufc.com|author=Staff|date=2013-09-09|access-date=2013-09-09}}{{cite web|url=http://www.mmajunkie.com/news/2013/09/mark-hunt-vs-antonio-silva-targeted-for-ufcs-december-return-to-australia|title=Mark Hunt vs. Antonio Silva targeted for UFC's December return to Australia|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|date=2013-09-04|access-date=2013-09-04|archive-date=4 September 2013|archive-url=https://archive.today/20130904221657/http://www.mmajunkie.com/news/2013/09/mark-hunt-vs-antonio-silva-targeted-for-ufcs-december-return-to-australia|url-status=dead}} The event was broadcast live on [[Fox Sports 1]].\n\n==Background==\nThe event was the first the UFC has hosted in [[Brisbane]].{{cite web|url=http://www.heraldsun.com.au/sport/boxing-mma/cagefighters-head-to-brisbane-for-ufc-event/story-fni2urrv-1226715009232|title=Cage-fighters head to Brisbane for UFC event |publisher=heraldsun.com.au|author=Chris Garry|date=2013-09-09|access-date=2013-09-09}} Overall, it was the fifth UFC event to be held in Australia.\n\nThe card was headlined by a heavyweight bout between [[Mark Hunt]] and [[Antônio Silva (fighter)|Antônio Silva]].{{cite web|url=http://www.theroar.com.au/2013/09/10/mark-hunt-to-headline-brisbane-ufc-card-in-december-interview/|title=Mark Hunt to headline Brisbane December UFC card |publisher=theroar.com.au|author=Jason Tuilo|date=2013-09-10|access-date=2013-09-10}} The main event ended in a rare majority draw decision; one judge scoring the fight 48–47 for Mark Hunt, while the other two saw it a 47–47 draw.{{cite web|url=http://www.mmadecisions.com/decision/4738/Antonio-Silva-vs-Mark-Hunt|title=Antonio Silva drew with Mark Hunt|publisher=mmadecisions.com.|author=Staff|date=2013-12-07|access-date=2013-12-07|url-status=dead|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20131217203442/http://www.mmadecisions.com/decision/4738/Antonio-Silva-vs-Mark-Hunt|archive-date=2013-12-17}} Post-fight, UFC President Dana White said that the bout had earned ''Fight of the Night'' honors.{{fact|date=November 2021}}\n\nSubsequently, Silva failed a post-fight test for elevated testosterone (he had been undergoing UFC-approved [[testosterone replacement therapy]]). Silva was suspended for nine months, retroactive to the date of the fight.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2013/12/antonio-bigfoot-silva-fails-post-fight-drug-test-stripped-of-50000-bonus/|title=Antonio Silva fails post-fight drug test|access-date=17 December 2013}} Due to Silva's positive test, Hunt was given the total of $100,000 from the ''Fight of the Night'' bonus award. For Hunt the bout result remains a \"draw\" on his official record, while in Silva's case it was overturned to a no-contest.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2013/12/antonio-bigfoot-silva-fails-post-fight-drug-test-stripped-of-50000-bonus/|title=Antonio Silva fails post-fight drug test|publisher=mmajunkie.com|access-date=17 December 2013}}\n\n[[Brian Melancon]] was expected to face [[Robert Whittaker (fighter)|Robert Whittaker]] at the event. However, Melancon pulled out of the bout and subsequently announced his retirement due to [[renal]] stress. As a result, Whittaker was removed from the card as well.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2013/11/20/brian-melancon-injury-scraps-bout-with-robert-whittaker-from-ufc-fight-night-33-in-australia/|title=Brian Melancon injury scraps bout with Robert Whittaker from UFC Fight Night 33 in Australia |publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Matt Erickson|date=2013-11-20|access-date=2013-11-20}}{{cite web|url=http://www.ufc.com/news/Melancon-Retires-at-31|title=Melancon retires at 31 |publisher=ufc.com|author=Staff|date=2013-11-20|access-date=2013-11-20}}\n\n[[Mitch Gagnon]] was expected to face [[Alex Caceres]] at the event. However, the bout was cancelled during the week leading up to the event due to an alleged [[visa (document)|visa]] issue for Gagnon, restricting his entry to Australia.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2013/12/alex-caceres-vs-mitch-gagnon-scratched-from-fridays-ufc-fight-night-33-main-card/|title=Alex Caceres vs. Mitch Gagnon scratched from Friday's UFC Fight Night 33 main card|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|date=2013-12-02|access-date=2013-12-02|archive-date=2 December 2013|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20131202150942/http://mmajunkie.com/2013/12/alex-caceres-vs-mitch-gagnon-scratched-from-fridays-ufc-fight-night-33-main-card/|url-status=dead}}\n\n==Results==\n{{MMAevent}}\n{{MMAevent card|Main Card (Fox Sports 1)}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Heavyweight\n|[[Mark Hunt]]\n|vs.\n|[[Antônio Silva (fighter)|Antônio Silva]]\n|Draw (majority) (48–47, 47–47, 47–47)\n|5\n|5:00\n|Silva tested positive for elevated levels of testosterone after the fight. [[UFC Fight Night: Hunt vs. Bigfoot]]\n|followingevent= [[UFC 168|UFC 168: Weidman vs. Silva 2]]\n}}\n'''''UFC on Fox: Johnson vs. Benavidez 2''''' (also known as '''''UFC on Fox 9''''') was a [[mixed martial arts]] event held on December 14, 2013, at the [[Sleep Train Arena]] in [[Sacramento, California]].{{cite web|url=http://www.mmajunkie.com/news/2013/09/ufc-on-fox-9-slated-for-dec-14-in-sacramento|title=UFC on FOX 9 slated for Dec. 14 in Sacramento|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|date=2013-09-04|access-date=2013-09-04|archive-date=2016-03-04|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20160304075725/http://www.mmajunkie.com/news/2013/09/ufc-on-fox-9-slated-for-dec-14-in-sacramento|url-status=dead}} It was the UFC's third visit to the venue, after [[UFC 65]] and [[UFC 73]].\n\n==Background==\nA [[UFC Lightweight Championship|UFC Lightweight title]] bout between the former champion [[Anthony Pettis]] and former top contender [[T. J. Grant]] was targeted for this event.{{cite web|url=https://bleacherreport.com/articles/1770710-dana-white-wants-anthony-pettis-vs-tj-grant-at-ufc-on-fox-9|title=Dana White Wants Anthony Pettis vs. T. J. Grant at UFC on Fox 9|publisher=bleacherreport.com|author=Sean Smith|date=2013-09-11|access-date=2013-09-11}} However, Grant revealed in mid-September that he has yet to be medically cleared after suffering a concussion in training and would not be eligible to compete on December 14 and opted to decline the bout.{{cite web|url=http://www.mmajunkie.com/news/2013/09/t-j-grant-ruled-out-of-planned-ufc-on-fox-9-title-fight-with-anthony-pettis|title=T.J. Grant ruled out of planned UFC on FOX 9 title fight with Anthony Pettis|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|date=2013-09-16|access-date=2013-09-16|archive-date=2013-09-20|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20130920012305/http://www.mmajunkie.com/news/2013/09/t-j-grant-ruled-out-of-planned-ufc-on-fox-9-title-fight-with-anthony-pettis|url-status=dead}} Pettis was then expected to face [[Josh Thomson]] in the event headliner.{{cite web|url=http://www.mmajunkie.com/news/2013/09/anthony-pettis-vs-josh-thomson-booked-for-ufc-on-fox-9-headliner|title=Anthony Pettis vs. Josh Thomson booked for UFC on FOX 9 headliner|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|date=2013-09-16|access-date=2013-09-16|archive-date=2013-09-20|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20130920000152/http://www.mmajunkie.com/news/2013/09/anthony-pettis-vs-josh-thomson-booked-for-ufc-on-fox-9-headliner|url-status=dead}} However, on November 10, 2013, it was announced that Pettis was injured and the bout was cancelled. The new main event was a rematch for the Flyweight title between [[Demetrious Johnson]] and [[Joseph Benavidez]].{{cite web|url=http://www.mmajunkie.com/news/2013/11/pettis-thomson-off-ufc-on-fox-9-johnson-benavidez-shifts-to-main-event|title=Pettis-Thomson off UFC on FOX 9, Johnson-Benavidez shifts to main event|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|date=2013-11-10|access-date=2013-11-10|archive-date=2013-11-11|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20131111063126/http://www.mmajunkie.com/news/2013/11/pettis-thomson-off-ufc-on-fox-9-johnson-benavidez-shifts-to-main-event|url-status=dead}}\n\nCo-featured on the card was a bout between top bantamweight contenders [[Urijah Faber]] and [[Michael McDonald (MMA fighter)|Michael McDonald]].{{cite web|url=https://bleacherreport.com/articles/1770749-urijah-faber-vs-michael-mcdonald-set-as-co-main-event-for-ufc-on-fox-9|title=Urijah Faber vs. Michael McDonald Set as Co-Main Event for UFC on Fox 9 |publisher=bleacherreport.com|author=Damon Martin|date=2013-09-11|access-date=2013-09-11}}\n\n[[Ian McCall (fighter)|Ian McCall]] was expected to face [[Scott Jorgensen]] at the event. However, McCall was forced out of the bout with an injury and replaced by [[John Dodson (fighter)|John Dodson]].{{cite web|url=http://themmareport.com/2013/10/with-ian-mccall-injured-scott-jorgensen-now-meets-john-dodson-at-ufc-on-fox-9/|title=With Ian McCall injured, Scott Jorgensen now meets John Dodson|publisher=themmareport.com|author=Jason Floyd|date=2013-10-29|access-date=2013-10-29}} Then on December 3, Dodson himself was forced from the bout after sustaining a knee injury.{{cite web|url=http://www.bloodyelbow.com/2013/12/3/5172562/report-john-dodson-out-of-ufc-on-fox-9-fight-against-scott-jorgensen-knee-injury-mma-news|title=John Dodson out of UFC on Fox 9 fight against Scott Jorgensen due to knee injury|publisher=bloodyelbow.com|author=Chris Hall|date=2013-12-03|access-date=2013-12-03|archive-date=2013-12-04|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20131204135230/http://www.bloodyelbow.com/2013/12/3/5172562/report-john-dodson-out-of-ufc-on-fox-9-fight-against-scott-jorgensen-knee-injury-mma-news|url-status=dead}} Jorgensen instead faced former [[Bellator MMA|Bellator Bantamweight Champion]] [[Zach Makovsky]].{{cite web|url=http://www.mmaweekly.com/rfa-flyweight-champion-zach-makovsky-tapped-to-face-scott-jorgensen-at-ufc-on-fox-9|title=RFA Flyweight Champion Zach Makovsky Tapped to Face Scott Jorgensen at UFC on Fox 9|publisher=mmaweekly.com|author=Ken Pishna|date=2013-12-04|access-date=2013-12-04}}\n\n[[Jamie Varner]] was expected to face [[Pat Healy (fighter)|Pat Healy]] at this event. However, Varner pulled out of the bout due to injury and was replaced by [[Bobby Green]]{{cite web|url=http://www.bloodyelbow.com/2013/11/12/5096306/ufc-on-fox-9-bobby-green-vs-pat-healy-varner-ufc-news|title=Bobby Green in for Jamie Varner, will face Pat Healy at UFC on Fox 9|publisher=bloodyelbow.com|author=Tim Burke|access-date=2013-11-12|archive-date=2015-09-24|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20150924004413/http://www.bloodyelbow.com/2013/11/12/5096306/ufc-on-fox-9-bobby-green-vs-pat-healy-varner-ufc-news|url-status=dead}}\n\n[[Kelvin Gastelum]] was expected to face [[Court McGee]] at this event. However, Gastelum pulled out of the bout, citing an undisclosed injury and was replaced by [[Ryan LaFlare]].{{cite web|url=http://www.bloodyelbow.com/2013/11/13/5099934/ryan-laflare-in-for-kelvin-gastelum-faces-court-mcgee-at-ufc-on-fox-9|title=Ryan LaFlare in for Kelvin Gastelum, faces Court McGee at UFC on Fox 9|publisher=bloodyelbow.com|author=Tim Burke|access-date=2013-11-13|archive-date=2013-11-13|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20131113205605/http://www.bloodyelbow.com/2013/11/13/5099934/ryan-laflare-in-for-kelvin-gastelum-faces-court-mcgee-at-ufc-on-fox-9|url-status=dead}}\n\n[[John Moraga]] was expected to face [[Darren Uyenoyama]] at the event. However, Moraga pulled out of the bout with an undisclosed injury and was replaced by promotional newcomer [[Alp Ozkilic]].{{cite web|url=http://www.mmafighting.com/2013/11/26/5148586/injury-forces-john-moraga-out-of-ufc-on-fox-9-bout-with-darren|title=Injury forces John Moraga out of UFC on FOX 9 bout with Darren Uyenoyama|publisher=mmafighting.com|author=Dave Doyle|access-date=2013-11-26}}\n\n[[Carlos Condit]] was expected to face [[Matt Brown (fighter)|Matt Brown]] in a welterweight bout on the main card. However, on December 7, 2013, it was announced that Brown had to pull out of the bout due to herniated discs in his back.{{cite web|url=http://www.mmatorch.com/artman2/publish/UFC_2/article_19501.shtml#.UqOJZ9JvIgw|title=Matt Brown pulls out of Carlos Condit fight at UFC on Fox 9 due to injury |publisher=mmatorch.com|author=Jamie Penick|access-date=2013-12-07}} Sources close to the promotion on Saturday, December 7, 2013, told MMAjunkie no replacement was sought to meet Condit on the card, thus removing the fight entirely from the event.{{cite web|title=Matt Brown injured, fight vs. Carlos Condit scrapped from UFC on FOX 9|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2013/12/matt-brown-injured-fight-vs-carlos-condit-scrapped-from-ufc-on-fox-9|publisher=MMA Junkie Staff|access-date=2013-12-07}}\n\nThis was the first MMA event held at the Sleep Train Arena since [[WEC 48]] on April 24, 2010, where Johnson, Faber, Mendes and Jorgensen also fought. Faber headlined or co-headlined each of the arena's previous four MMA events.[http://www.wrestlingdata.com/index.php?befehl=shows&sort=ort&land=3®ion=359&stadt=248&arena=12732 Events at Sleep Train mixed martial arts event in 2014}}\n{{Infobox MMA event\n|name=UFC Fight Night: Saffiedine vs. Lim\n|image=New UFN 34 poster.jpg\n|promotion=[[Ultimate Fighting Championship]]\n|date= January 4, debut|publisher=MMAjunkie.com|author=Staff|date=2014-01-04|access-date=2014-01-04|archive-date=2023-04-08|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20230408112158/http://mmajunkie.com/2014/01/ufc-fight-night-34-draws-reported-5216-attendance-in-singapore-debut/|url-status=dead}}\n|previousevent= [[UFC 168|UFC 168: Weidman vs. Silva 2]]\n|followingevent= [[UFC Fight Night: Rockhold vs. Philippou]]\n}}\n\n'''''UFC Fight Night: Saffiedine vs. Lim''''' (also known as '''''UFC Fight Night 34''''') was a [[mixed martial arts]] event held on January 4, 2014, at the [[Marina Bay Sands]] in [[Marina Bay, Singapore]].{{cite web|url=http://www.mmajunkie.com/news/2013/08/ufc-announces-jan-4-for-debut-event-in-singapore|title=UFC announces Jan. 4 for debut event in Singapore|publisher=MMAjunkie.com|author=Staff|date=2013-09-16|access-date=2013-09-16|archive-date=2022-09-06|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20220906213015/http://www.mmajunkie.com/news/2013/08/ufc-announces-jan-4-for-debut-event-in-singapore|url-status=dead}}\n\n==Background==\nThis event was the first event the UFC held in [[Singapore]].{{cite news | url=https://www.straitstimes.com/sport/mma-ufc-to-host-first-event-in-singapore-since-2014 | title=MMA: UFC to return to Singapore on June 17 after three-year absence | newspaper=The Straits Times | date=23 February 2017 | access-date=13 December 2018 | last1=Chia | first1=Alvin }} It was the first ''Fight Night'' card that wasn't aired on television in the [[United States]] market. Instead, the UFC offered fans based in North America, Australia and New Zealand the option to watch it via a new subscription service dubbed \"[[UFC Fight Pass]]\".{{cite web|url=https://www.usatoday.com/story/sports/ufc/2013/12/02/ufc-to-unveil-digital-network-for-global-expansion-/3797951/|title=UFC to unveil digital network for global expansion|publisher=USAtoday.com|author=Staff|date=2013-12-02|access-date=2013-12-02}}\n\nThe event was expected to be headlined by a welterweight bout between [[Jake Ellenberger]] and [[Tarec Saffiedine]]. However, in late November, Ellenberger pulled out of the bout citing an injury and was replaced by [[Lim Hyun-gyu]].{{cite web|url=http://www.mmafighting.com/2013/11/30/5160544/jake-ellenberger-reveals-hamstring-injury-forced-him-out-of-ufc-fight|title=Jake Ellenberger reveals hamstring injury forced him out of UFC Fight Night 34|publisher=MMAjfighting.com.|author=Dave Doyle|date=2013-11-30|access-date=2013-11-30}} Lim's original opponent, [[Kiichi Kunimoto]] instead faced Luiz Dutra Jr.{{cite web|url=http://fightsportasia.com/2013/11/29/luiz-dutra-jr-replaces-lim-faces-kiichi-kunimoto-at-ufc-fight-night-34/|title=Luiz Dutra Jr. replaces Lim, meets Kiichi Kunimoto at UFC Fight Night 34|publisher=fightsportasia.com.com|author=Staff|date=2013-11-29|access-date=2013-11-29|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20131129055238/http://fightsportasia.com/2013/11/29/luiz-dutra-jr-replaces-lim-faces-kiichi-kunimoto-at-ufc-fight-night-34/|archive-date=2013-11-29|url-status=dead}}\n\n[[Hacran Dias]] was expected to face [[Tatsuya Kawajiri]] at the event. However, Dias pulled out of the bout citing an injury.{{cite web|url=http://www.mmafighting.com/2013/11/30/5160262/hacran-dias-injured-out-of-ufc-fight-night-34-co-main-event|title=Hacran Dias injured, out of UFC Fight Night 34 co-main event|publisher=mmafighting.com|author=Guilherme Cruz|date=2013-11-30|access-date=2013-11-30}} Kawajiri instead faced promotional newcomer [[Sean Soriano]].{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2013/12/tatsuya-kawajiri-welcomes-newcomer-sean-soriano-to-octagon-at-ufc-fight-night-34/|title=Tatsuya Kawajiri welcomes newcomer Sean Soriano to octagon this card.{{cite web|url=http://www.bloodyelbow.com/2013/11/13/5098692/ufc-will-chope-vs-bekbulat-magomedov-singapore|title=UFC signs 'World's Tallest Featherweight' Will Chope, set to take on fellow Holloway]].{{cite web|url=http://www.bloodyelbow.com/ufc-fight-night-singapore/2013/11/21/5128968/will-chope-vs-max-hollaway-ufc-fight-night-34-singapore-asia|title=Will Chope vs. Max Holloway signed [[UFC Fight Night: Saffiedine vs. Lim]]\n|followingevent= [[UFC on Fox: Henderson vs. Thomson]]\n}}\n\n'''''UFC Fight Night: Rockhold vs. Philippou''''' (also known as '''''UFC Fight Night 35''''') was a [[mixed martial arts]] event held on January 15, 2014, at [[The Arena at Gwinnett Center]] in [[Duluth, Georgia]].{{cite web|url=http://www.ufc.com/news/ufc-returning-to-atlanta-2014|title=UFC returns to Atlanta January 15|publisher=ufc.com.com|author=Staff|date=2013-11-14|access-date=2013-11-14}} The event was broadcast live on [[Fox Sports 1]], and was a live lead in to the pilot episode of [[The Ultimate Fighter Nations: Canada vs. Australia]].\n\n==Background==\nThe event was headlined by a middleweight bout between [[Luke Rockhold]] and [[Costas Philippou]].{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2013/11/17/ufc-fight-night-35-in-georgia-adds-luke-rockhold-constantinos-philippou-as-main-event/|title=UFC Fight Night 35 in Georgia adds Luke Rockhold-Constantinos Philippou as main event|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|date=2013-11-17|access-date=2013-11-17}}\n\n[[Thiago Silva (fighter)|Thiago Silva]] was briefly linked to a bout with [[Ovince St. Preux]] at the event. However, Silva was quickly removed from the bout for an undisclosed injury.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2013/11/21/thiago-silva-scratches-from-ufc-fight-night-35-co-main-opposite-ovince-st-preux/|title=Thiago Silva scratches from UFC Fight Night 35 co-main opposite Ovince St. Preux|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|date=2013-11-21|access-date=2013-11-21|archive-date=2013-11-23|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20131123193929/http://mmajunkie.com/2013/11/21/thiago-silva-scratches-from-ufc-fight-night-35-co-main-opposite-ovince-st-preux/|url-status=dead}} The bout was rescheduled and was expected to take place on March 15, 2014, at [[UFC 171]], before being cancelled entirely after Silva was arrested and released from the UFC.{{cite web|url=http://www.5thround.com/160448/thiago-silva-vs-ovince-st-preux-re-booked-for-ufc-171/|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20131216071452/http://www.5thround.com/160448/thiago-silva-vs-ovince-st-preux-re-booked-for-ufc-171/|url-status=usurped|archive-date=December 16, 2013|title=Thiago Silva vs. Ovince St. Preux Re-Booked for UFC 171|publisher=5thround.com|date=2013-12-07|access-date=2013-12-07|author=Tom Ngo}}\n\n[[Jason High]] was expected to face [[Adlan Amagov]] at the event. However, Amagov was forced out of the bout due to injury and was replaced by promotional newcomer [[Beneil Dariush]].{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2013/12/jason-high-announces-hes-fighting-newcomer-beneil-dariush-at-ufc-fight-night-35/|title=Jason High announces he's fighting newcomer Baneil Dariush at UFC Fight Night 35|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|date=2013-12-20|access-date=2013-12-20|archive-date=2023-07-20|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20230720151339/http://mmajunkie.com/2013/12/jason-high-announces-hes-fighting-newcomer-beneil-dariush-at-ufc-fight-night-35/|url-status=dead}} In turn, High was forced out of the bout with appendicitis and was replaced by returning veteran [[Charlie Brenneman]].{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2014/01/jason-high-out-of-ufc-fight-night-35-charlie-brenneman-returns-to-meet-beneil-dariush/|title=Jason High out of UFC Fight Night 35, Charlie Brenneman returns to meet Beneil Dariush|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|date=2013-01-02|access-date=2013-01-02|archive-date=2014-01-02|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20140102195850/http://mmajunkie.com/2014/01/jason-high-out-of-ufc-fight-night-35-charlie-brenneman-returns-to-meet-beneil-dariush/|url-status=dead}}\n\n==Results==\n{{MMAevent}}\n{{MMAevent card|Main Card (Fox Sports 1)}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Middleweight\n|[[Luke Rockhold]]\n|def.\n|[[Costas Philippou]]\n|TKO (body kick)\n|1\n|2:31\n|\n}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Middleweight\n|[[Brad Tavares]]\n|def.\n|[[Lorenz Larkin]]\n|Decision (unanimous) (29–28, 29–28, 29–28)\n|3\n|5:00\n|\n}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Bantamweight\n|[[T.J. However, both were forced off the card due to undisclosed circumstances.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2014/01/pascal-krauss-vs-adam-khaliev-scrapped-from-ufc-on-fox-10-in-chicago/|title=Pascal Krauss vs. Adam Khaliev scrapped from UFC on FOX 10 in Chicago|publisher=MMAjunkie.com|author=Matt Erickson|access-date=2014-01-11|archive-date=2014-01-12|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20140112062518/http://mmajunkie.com/2014/01/pascal-krauss-vs-adam-khaliev-scrapped-from-ufc-on-fox-10-in-chicago/|url-status=dead}}\n\nUFC on FOX 10 had 2.55 million viewers, which was up from 2.44 million for UFC on FOX 9.{{cite web|url= http://mmanuts.com/news/ufc-on-fox-10-ratings/ |title= UFC on Fox 10 Picks up 2.55 Million Viewers |publisher=mmanuts.com|author=Dave Walsh |date= January 27, 2014|access-date= January 27, 2014}}\n\n==Results==\n{{MMAevent}}\n{{MMAevent card|Main Card (Fox)}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Lightweight\n|[[Benson Henderson]]\n|def.\n|[[Josh Thomson]]\n|Decision (split) (47–48, 48–47, 49–46)\n|5\n|5:00\n|\n}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Heavyweight\n|[[Stipe Miocic]]\n|def.\n|[[Gabriel Gonzaga]]\n|Decision (unanimous) (30–27, 30–27, 29–28)\n|3\n|5:00\n|\n}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Lightweight\n|[[Donald Cerrone]]\n|def.\n|[[Adriano Martins]]\n|KO (head kick)\n|1\n|4:40\n|\n}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Featherweight\n|[[Jeremy Stephens]]\n|def.\n|[[Darren Elkins]]\n|Decision (unanimous) (29–28, 30–27, 30–27)\n|3\n|5:00\n|\n}}\n{{MMAevent card|Preliminary card (Fox Sports 1)|header=no}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Bantamweight\n|[[Alex Caceres]]\n|def.\n|[[Sergio Pettis]]\n|Submission (rear-naked choke)\n|3\n|4:39\n|\n}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Bantamweight\n|[[Eddie Wineland]]\n|def.\n|[[Yves Jabouin]]\n|TKO (punches)\n|2\n|4:16\n|\n}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Bantamweight\n|[[Chico Camus]]\n|vs.\n|[[Yaotzin Meza]]\n|No Contest (overturned)\n|3\n|5:00\n|This bout was ruled a No Contest due to Camus testing positive for marijuana in the post fight drug test. China","revisions":[{"slots":{"main":{"contentmodel":"wikitext","contentformat":"text/x-wiki","content":"{{short description|UFC mixed martial arts television series and event in 2014}}\n{{Infobox television\n| image = \n| image_size = \n| caption =\n|genre = [[reality television|Reality]], [[Broadcasting of sports events|Sports]]\n| runtime = 60 minutes\n| composer =\n| creator = [[Frank Fertitta III]], [[Lorenzo Fertitta]], [[Dana White]]\n| executive_producer = \n| starring = Dana White, [[Cung Le]], [[Tiequan Zhang]], Hailin Ao\n| country = [[China]]\n| network = [[Liaoning Television|Liaoning Satellite TV]]\n| company = \n| first_aired = {{Start date|2013|12|07}}\n| last_aired = {{End date|2014|01|06}}\n| num_episodes = \n}}\n\t\n'''''The Ultimate Fighter: China''''' is an international installment of the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]] (UFC)-produced [[reality television]] series ''[[The Ultimate Fighter]]''. It is the fourth season produced outside the United States and the first to air in China, on [[Liaoning Television]] starting December 7, 2013.{{cite web |url=http://www.mixedmartialarts.com/mma.cfm?go=news.detail&gid=436711 |title=TUF 18 starts Wed 9/4 at 10 PM on Fox Sports 1 |publisher=mixedmartialarts.com |date=April 15, 2013 |access-date=August 23, 2013}}\n\nUFC officially announced the season in June 2013.{{cite web |url=http://mmajunkie.com/2013/06/29/the-ultimate-fighter-china-tryouts-announced-for-july-and-august/ |title=‘The Ultimate Fighter: China’ tryouts announced for July and August |publisher=MMAJunkie.com |date=June 29, 2013 |access-date=November 16, 2013}} Tryouts were held in July and August 2013 in [[Beijing]], [[Singapore]] and [[Macao]]. Fighters were looked at in the [[featherweight (MMA)|featherweight]], [[lightweight (MMA)|lightweight]] and [[welterweight (MMA)|welterweight]] divisions, with over 150 applicants. The featherweight and welterweight classes were ultimately chosen. Sixteen fighters competed in two single-elimination tournaments. The final bouts aired live on March 1, 2014.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2013/11/16/the-ultimate-fighter-china-cast-revealed-series-debuts-dec-7/ |title=‘The Ultimate Fighter: China’ cast revealed, series debuts Dec. 7 |publisher=MMAJunkie.com |date=November 16, 2013 |access-date=November 16, 2013 |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20131118202612/http://mmajunkie.com/2013/11/16/the-ultimate-fighter-china-cast-revealed-series-debuts-dec-7/ |archive-date=November 18, 2013 }} Vietnamese-American [[Cung Le]] was the show's mentor and chief coach. UFC fighter [[Tiequan Zhang]] and Chinese mixed martial artist Hailin Ao coached the two teams.\n\n==Cast==\n\n===Chief staff===\n*[[Cung Le]], mentor and chief coach\n*Scott Sheeley, assistant coach\n\n===Coaches===\n{{Col-begin}}\n{{Col-break}}\n;{{color box|red|border=silver}} {{color box|yellow|border=silver}} '''Team Sky Dragons'''\n*[[Tiequan Zhang]], Head coach\n*Bao Li Gao, Sanda coach\n*Vince Soberano, Muay Thai coach\n*Flavio Bueno, jiu jitsu coach\n{{Col-break}}\n;{{color box|yellow|border=silver}} {{color box|black|border=silver}} '''Team Flying Lions'''\n*Hailin Ao, head coach (Episodes 1–4)\n*An Hu, Sanda coach\n*Marco Machado, jiu jitsu coach\n*Fred Thomassen, assistant coach\n*Scott Sheeley, assistant coach (Episode 5-)\n*[[Alexander Gustafsson]], guest coach\n{{col-end}}\n\n===Fighters===\n*'''Team Sky Dragons'''{{cite web |url=http://www.ufc.com/news/tuf-china-cast-announcement |title=TUF China Cast Announced |publisher=ufc.com |date=November 15, 2013 |access-date=November 16, 2013}}\n**'''Featherweights:''' [[Ning Guangyou]], Fu Changxin, [[Yao Zhikui]], and He Jianwei.\n**'''Welterweights:''' [[Zhang Lipeng]], Albert Cheng, Wu Qize, and Li Jinying*.\n\n''*Li quit on episode 2. He was replaced by Fu Ziyi.''\n\n*'''Team Flying Lions'''\n**'''Featherweights:''' Yang Jianping, Rocky Lee, Allen Chong, and Shih Liang.\n**'''Welterweights:''' Wang Sai, Wang Anying, Zhu Qingxiang, and Dong Xin*.\n\n''*Dong was replaced by Yong Shun on episode 5 due to injury.''\n\n===Referees===\n*[[John Sharp (referee)|John Sharp]]\n\n==Episodes==\n\nThere will be a total of 12 episodes aired on Liaoning Satellite TV, for Season 1. Each episode is one hour long (including advertisements), with a short commentary (less than 10 minutes) following directly after the episode. TUF: China is scheduled to air every Saturday at 22:00 Beijing Time, and beginning January 2014, it will air every Saturday and Sunday, consecutively (also at the same time). The series will end on January 26, 2014.[http://www.lntv.com.cn/html/News/1/1_6050.html \"The Ultimate Fighter: China\" on December 7, Liaoning TV Premiere]\n\n;'''Episode 1: Premiere (December 7, 2013)'''\n*The episode starts with an explanation about the UFC and the Ultimate Fighter (for those who are not familiar with it).\n*The coaches enter the Ultimate Fighter training center; first one is [[Tiequan Zhang]] and then Hailin Ao. [[Cung Le]] shows up and explain them the goals of this season of the Ultimate Fighter.\n*The fighters check the training center by themselves and start training with some equipment. The coaches arrive and explain them what the UFC is and also remind them of how important this opportunity is for the history of MMA in China and Asia.\n*Preliminary training matches are held to gauge each fighter's strengths and weaknesses in order to determine the fighter picks made for the two teams: Team Sky Dragons and Team Flying Lions (led by Tiequan Zhang and Hailin Ao, respectively).\n*Team Sky Dragons and Team Flying Lions then picked teams and Le flipped a coin (red for the Sky Dragons, yellow for the Flying Lions). Ao won the coin toss and opted to choose the first fighter.\n*'''Featherweight selection:'''\n{| class=\"wikitable\"\n|-\n!Team\n!1st Pick\n!2nd Pick\n!3rd Pick\n!4th Pick\n|-\n!Flying Lions\n|Yang Jianping\n|Rocky Lee\n|Allen Chong\n|Shih Liang\n|-\n!Sky Dragons\n|Ning Guangyou\n|Fu Changxin\n|Yao Zhikui\n|He Jianwei\n|}\n\n*'''Welterweight selection:'''\n{| class=\"wikitable\"\n|-\n!Team\n!1st Pick\n!2nd Pick\n!3rd Pick\n!4th Pick\n|-\n!Flying Lions\n|Wang Sai\n|Wang Anying\n|Zhu Qingxiang\n|Dong Xin\n|-\n!Sky Dragons\n|Zhang Lipeng\n|Albert Cheng\n|Wu Qize\n|Li Jinying\n|}\n*Le announces that fighters could receive $25,000 for ''Knockout of the Season'', ''Submission of the Season'' and ''Fight of the Season''.\n*The teams visit their locker rooms and Le visits both of them to remind the teams of focusing on developing together during the season.\n\n;'''Episode 2: (December 14, 2013)'''\n*The fighters from Team Flying Lions arrive at the house and explore the different bedrooms while they decide which one they should choose. In Team Sky Dragons' van ride to the House, Li Jinying talks about being a guy only used to fitness and that he joined MMA after seeing the fighters in magazines. His teammates tell him that they will help him adjust to the MMA life. He also discusses being the last pick of the team.\n*Team Sky Dragons finally arrive at the house and they start interacting with the other fighters.\n*During the training session of the Sky Dragons, Li reveals it was his first training and that he could not do anything but defend himself. Back in the house he discusses with a teammate who would choose to fight him and he's told that he should focus on developing his game and not letting the others know his weaknesses as they have the fight pick and they'll try to avoid his fight as long as possible.\n*In their next session, Li still struggles with training and is unable to throw or take strikes. Cung Le is not satisfied with what he sees and the coaches discuss Li's future. Le challenges him to stay by doing two 3-minute rounds, but Li says he cannot do it because of his relationships with his teammates. Le tells him to choose one of the coaches then.\n*After being easily dominated by Le's assistant coach Scott Sheeley, Li decides to quit. Le says that this is the best decision and that they will decide between the alternates who will replace him.\n*Team Flying Lions finally starts training and Ao discusses how important it is to train every aspect of the game and with that they will be ready for the fights. He also introduces his coaching staff for the show.\n*Le meets with Fu Ziyi and tells him that he's on the show as a replacement. He is very excited to be there and Zhang welcomes him.\n*The first match-up is chosen by Tiequan Zhang: Team Sky Dragons' Zhang Lipeng vs. Team Flying Lions' Zhu Qingxiang. During the square off, Zhu pushed Zhang away.\n*Coach Zhang compliments Zhang Lipeng's skills and comprehension to learn. Later, Zhu confesses that he pushed Zhang because he felt uncomfortable during their stare down.\n*Zhang Lipeng said that he trained with Hailin Ao in the past and he feels that the coach really wants to see him defeated. He also reflected on how many mistakes he made in his career when he was younger and that now he has a better record and technique.\n*Former [[List of UFC champions#Lightweight Championship|UFC Lightweight Champion]] [[Benson Henderson]] made a special visit and watched the fight.\n*'''Zhang Lipeng''' defeated Zhu Qingxiang via submission (punches) in round 1.\n*After the fight, Le admitted that he was impressed with the back and forth action, especially because it was the first fight of the season. He praised Zhu's attitude and said he could do well with the right training.\n*Zhang chooses the next match-up: Team Sky Dragons' Ning Guangyou vs. Team Flying Lions' Shih Liang.\n\n;'''Episode 3: (December 21, 2013)'''\n*Cung Le and the coaches bring some beer for the fighters while they gather together to watch [[UFC 166]].\n*The fight pick from last episode is shown again and both fighters analyze how they should perform to win it. Hailin Ao feels that this will be a good match as Shih likes to strike and Ning prefers to wrestle. Tiequan Zhang also thinks that Ning is a good fighter and he's a good example for the rest of the team because of his commitment to training.\n*Ben Henderson helped Team Sky Dragons with some wrestling training and complimented Ning's skills.\n*[[Huang Jianxiang]], the show's host, decided to make a prank with Team Sky Dragons. He pretended to be the replacement and welcomed the Flying Lions after their training session.\n*Ning and his teammates discuss Shih's skills. Meanwhile, Team Flying Lions writes supportive messages for Shih and help him launch a [[Sky lantern|Kongming lantern]].\n*During the weigh-in, Chong called out He Jianwei.\n*Coach Vince Soberano reveals that Shih was his student for 3 years, and Ning is his student for one year. He is having a hard time watching the fight because of his feelings for both fighters. He goes to the locker room before the fight and does not watch it.\n*'''Ning Guangyou''' defeated Shih Liang via knockout (punch) in round 2.\n\n;'''Episode 4: (December 28, 2013)'''\n*Zhang chooses the next match-up: Team Sky Dragons' Albert Cheng vs. Team Flying Lions' Dong Xin.\n*Hailin Ao visited the house to support his fighters, specially Shih, who lost his fight. He gave them red [[hot water bottle]]s to play around with in training and warm themselves. The color was specially chosen to pick on the Sky Dragons team.\n*Allen Chong believes that Team Sky Dragons probably chose Dong because he lacks jiu jitsu skills to compete against Cheng.\n*Before the weigh-in, Team Flying Lions was uncomfortable with Ao's absence. After the weigh-in, Le brought them to their locker room and revealed that Ao had to leave the show for personal reasons. Therefore, the three assistant coaches are now responsible for making the decisions together.\n*'''Dong Xin''' defeated Albert Cheng via TKO (punches) in round 2.\n\n;'''Episode 5: (January 4, 2014)'''\n*Dong Xin enters the training center along with coach An Hu. Cung Le is waiting there with a doctor, whom explains to Dong that the results of his exams after his fight revealed a broken hand. He will be unable to continue training and fighting for the rest of the show. His teammates give him support and Le mentions that he will recommend him to fight in the live finale. After Dong leaves, Le mentions that his assistant Scott Sheeley will help them with training and will be in their corner. He will only work with them and won't interact with Team Sky Dragons' training.\n*An chooses the next match-up: Team Flying Lions' Yang Jianping vs. Team Sky Dragons' Fu Changxin.\n*Le welcomes Dong's replacement Yong Shun to Team Flying Lions. He mentions later that he doesn't know anything about him and he noticed that he is a very quiet person. It is not revealed if he will enter directly to the semi-finals or if he will have to fight before.\n*Fu mentions that he wanted to fight Yang because of Yang's big experience as a fighter, while Yang mentions that he wanted Fu because he's not a strong fighter. He also says that he admires Fu's courage during the fight pick to raise his hand as a sign to fight him.\n*Team Sky Dragons shows up with a special cake to celebrate He Jianwei's birthday.\n*Famous actor [[Daniel Wu]] made a special visit and watched the fight.\n*'''Yang Jianping''' defeated Fu Changxin via submission (armbar) in round 1.\n\n;'''Episode 6: (January 5, 2014)'''\n*Fu Ziyi reveals that he wants to fight Wang Sai next because of Wang's fame around Chinese MMA and that he just wants to challenge himself. He mentions that being in the octagon is worth even being beaten by Wang.\n*Wang Sai says that he knows the other team watches videos from the fighters of him team to discover weaknesses, but he believes that he barely has weaknesses and it will be hard for them to find out.\n*Fu mentions that he never received such amount of professional training since he was 20 years old (He is 35 now). Later, some scenes reveal that he was cutting weight already. even though the next fight was not announced. He says that this might be the last chance of his life and that he will fight regardless of how he is feeling.\n*An chooses the next match-up: Team Flying Lions' Wang Sai vs. Team Sky Dragons' Wu Qize. Fu seems to be surprised that he wasn't picked to fight now. Tiequan Zhang said that they were worried about Fu's weight and they made him cut weight because of that. He also says that he's surprised with the fight pick.\n*Wang decides to joke around with the other fighters by wearing a red shirt and pretend he's fighting his own teammates in the house. Some of the other fighters believe that his behavior sometimes is annoying.\n*Wu reveals that he already faced Wang in the past and what his opponent does not know is that he exercised a lot ever since and have made great progress. He says he is relaxed and the pressure is on Wang to perform due to being more famous than him.\n*Team Flying Lions made a prank on Team Sky Dragons' locker room with pink balloons and other pink objects.\n*Wang heard that Wu's skills are better now than they were 2 or 3 years ago when they trained together and he hopes that's true so he can face a better opponent in the octagon and show his abilities.\n*'''Wang Sai''' defeated Wu Qize via TKO (strikes) in round 2.\n*The coaches praised the fight, specially Wu's \"warrior's heart\". Cung Le agrees with them and also points out that this fight was an example that the fighters are learning what they have been taught.\n\n;'''Episode 7: (January 11, 2014)'''\n*Cung Le calls a meeting with both teams to reveal he was informed of someone from Team Flying Lions that went to their old hotel to pick up a package. He asks if the person who did it will step up and reveal himself for the rest of cast. Wang reveals that he went there to get his sports drink.\n*Le says that it takes a lot to step up and admit it, but that it is not all that he knows. He shows an [[iPhone]] and asks who owns it. Yang Jianping raises his hand.\n*During a confessional, Yang said that being away from everything is hard and he brought his phone so he could keep in touch with the outside world. He admitted it was his fault because they signed a confidentiality agreement before joining the house.\n*Back to the current moment, Le says a punishment is required for this situation and then shifts the fight pick to the hands of Team Sky Dragons.\n*Shih Liang reveals that it was Allen Chong that told someone from the other team about this secret and then that person reported it to one of the coaches, whom informed Le of the fact.\n*In the locker room, the team is arguing about keeping their secrets to themselves and Chong feels that they are blaming him for the incident. He replied during a confessional that he is only playing by the rules, while other people are not.\n*Wu Qize's teammates lifted him up when he got back to the house. He says that this shows they really are a team and all of his teammates praise his efforts in his fight. Wang Sai also went there to compliment him.\n*Zhang chooses the next match-up: Team Sky Dragons' Yao Zhikui vs. Team Flying Lions' Allen Chong.\n*Chong decides not to do heavy training along with the other fighters because he still has an injured neck and will fight within a day or two, so he prefers to tread lightly.\n*Inside the van, Wang and Yang have an argument after something Wang said about Yang that the latter did not like. Back in the house, An Hu tries to talk with both of them and revert the bad situation.\n*Chong teammates think he has little chance of winning the fight because he is only good at jiu-jitsu. Later, he talks briefly about [[Malaysia]]'s culture when it comes to fighting and struggles when he remembers his wife back home. Sheeley agrees to corner him for this fight and help him, though he confesses later that he wished Chong trained more the last days but he understands some of the reasons that Chong told them.\n*Fu Ziyi mentions that he is struggling with weight cut and he doesn't have enough energy to cut it for his fight in a few days. He decides to keep trying after Tiequan Zhang shows him some support.\n*'''Yao Zhikui''' defeated Allen Chong via knockout (strikes) in round 1.\n\n;'''Episode 8: (January 12, 2014)'''\n*The fighters from Team Sky Dragons had a problem with their weight scale and tried to get the other team's borrowed. After they were denied, some of them decide to search Team Flying Lion's locker room for it. Fu Ziyi manages to find the other team's scale and borrow it secretly.\n*Fu is still struggling to cut weight and thinks about quitting. Cung Le advises him not to do it because it would take a toll on his career. Fu decides to keep trying. Later though Le reveals that Fu won't be able to make weight and the UFC wants him to be removed from the show. They will decide whether he will be replaced by an alternate or someone from his team. Fu says goodbye to his teammates and leaves. Le goes to the other team's locker room and gives them the news. Wang Anying says that he will fight anybody, but he feels that is unfair to cut weight and not fight.\n*Le calls a meeting at the training center to announce that Wang Anying will advance directly to the semi-finals and Yong Shun will face Albert Cheng for Dong Xin's spot. Wang Anying feels good about advancing directly though he feels that it's not a good thing to advance without the experience of a first fight.\n*Yong feels worried because he wasn't prepared to fight right now as Wang Anying was already going to fight. Meanwhile, Tiequan Zhang decides to show a video of Cheng's girlfriend to him as a special motivation for his fight.\n*Le brings UFC light heavyweight [[Alexander Gustafsson]] to Team Flying Lions' training and he helps them with takedown defense.\n*'''Albert Cheng''' defeated Yong Shun via submission (strikes) in round 2.\n*During the fight, Yong grabbed the cage deliberately in two moments and received a one-point deduction.\n\n;'''Episode 9: (January 18, 2014)'''\n*The last featherweight fight of the first round is announced: Team Sky Dragons' He Jianwei vs. Team Flying Lions' Rocky Lee.\n*A doctor checks Lee's calf after he complained of pain in that area during training and thinks that he might have torn his muscle. He gives Lee some medicine and advises him to put some ice on it so it heals faster.\n*Both teams have a chance to relax and take part in a [[bowling]] challenge with every participant bowling one round and later the coaches for the final round. Team Sky Dragons wins and punishes the other team with the task of walking around with a bowling ball between their legs back and forth.\n*After a sparing session with Zhang Lipeng, Cung Le compliments his skills and desire to learn, also stating that he is the most well rounded welterweight in the season.\n*'''Rocky Lee''' defeated He Jianwei via submission (armbar) in round 1.\n\n;'''Episode 10: (January 19, 2014)'''\n*This episode is a recap of all the fights and events leading up to the semi-finals.\n\n;'''Episode 11: (January 25, 2014)'''\n*The first semi final matchups for both weight classes are announced: Wang Sai vs. Wang Anying and Yang Jianping vs. Yao Zhikui. To the fighters' surprise, the welterweight tournament will feature teammates matchups in the semifinals.\n*Concerned about an injured hand, Wang Sai prefers to avoid a visit to the hospital as he could find himself removed from his fight due to the injury.\n*'''Wang Sai''' defeated Wang Anying via submission (rear-naked choke) in round 2.\n*Both Yang and Yao reveal their thoughts about each other and their feelings regarding the important fight coming up.\n*'''Yang Jianping ''' defeated Yao Zhikui via unanimous decision in three rounds.\n\n;'''Episode 12 Season End: (January 26, 2014)'''\n*The Coaches' Challenge features a [[fishing]] style challenge with both coaches dressed as [[rikishi]]s. With An Hu's victory, he received $5,000 and each fighter of his team won [[renminbi|¥]]5,000.\n*'''Zhang Lipeng''' defeated Albert Cheng via submission (kimura) in round 2.\n*During Ning's preparation for his fight against Lee, [[Jung Chan Sung|Chan Sung Jung]] helped him with takedown defense.\n*'''Ning Guangyou''' defeated Rocky Lee via knockout (punch) in round 1.\n*Le and the coaches celebrate their time together on the show with a special speech and the finalists have a chance to add their photo to the tournament bracket.\n\n==Tournament brackets==\n\n===Welterweight bracket===\n{{Round8\n\n\n||{{color box|red|border=silver}} Wu Qize|2|{{color box|yellow|border=silver}} '''Wang Sai'''|'''TKO'''\n||{{color box|red|border=silver}} Fu Ziyi|-|{{color box|yellow|border=silver}} '''Wang Anying'''|'''*'''\n||{{color box|red|border=silver}} '''Zhang Lipeng'''|'''SUB'''|{{color box|yellow|border=silver}} Zhu Qingxiang|1\n||{{color box|red|border=silver}} Albert Cheng|2|{{color box|yellow|border=silver}} '''Dong Xin'''|'''TKO'''\n\n||{{color box|yellow|border=silver}} '''Wang Sai'''|'''SUB'''|{{color box|yellow|border=silver}} Wang Anying|2\n||{{color box|red|border=silver}} '''Zhang Lipeng'''|'''SUB'''|{{color box|red|border=silver}} Albert Cheng**|2\n\n||{{color box|red|border=silver}} '''Zhang Lipeng'''|'''SD'''|{{color box|yellow|border=silver}} Wang Sai|3\n|widescore=yes|3rdplace=no|RD1=Elimination Round|RD3=Finale}}\n\n''*Fu was unable to make weight and was removed from the show. In order to decide who would take Dong's spot in the semi-finals, Yong faced Cheng and lost by submission (punches) in round 2.''\n\n===Featherweight bracket===\n{{Round8\n\n\n||{{color box|red|border=silver}} Fu Changxin|1|{{color box|yellow|border=silver}} '''Yang Jianping'''|'''SUB'''\n||{{color box|red|border=silver}} '''Yao Zhikui'''|'''KO'''|{{color box|yellow|border=silver}} Allen Chong|1\n||{{color box|red|border=silver}} '''Ning Guangyou'''|'''KO'''|{{color box|yellow|border=silver}} Shih Liang|2\n||{{color box|red|border=silver}} He Jianwei|1|{{color box|yellow|border=silver}} '''Rocky Lee'''|'''SUB'''\n\n||{{color box|yellow|border=silver}} '''Yang Jianping'''|'''UD'''|{{color box|red|border=silver}} Yao Zhikui|3\n||{{color box|red|border=silver}} '''Ning Guangyou'''|'''KO'''|{{color box|yellow|border=silver}} Rocky Lee|1\n\n||{{color box|yellow|border=silver}} Yang Jianping|3|{{color box|red|border=silver}} '''Ning Guangyou'''|'''UD'''\n|widescore=yes|3rdplace=no|RD1=Elimination Round|RD3=Finale}}\n\n*''The bout between Yang Jianping and Ning Guangyou took place at [[UFC Fight Night: Bisping vs. Le]].''\n\n{| style=\"border:1px solid black;\" align=left\n|+ '''Legend'''\n|-\n| style=\"background:red; width:40px;\" |\n|  \n| Team Sky Dragons\n|-\n| style=\"background:yellow; width:40px;\" |\n|  \n| Team Flying Lions\n|-\n| style=\"width:60px;\" | {{center|UD}}\n|  \n| Unanimous Decision\n|-\n| style=\"width:60px;\" | {{center|SD}}\n|  \n| Split Decision\n|-\n| style=\"width:60px;\" | {{center|SUB}}\n|  \n| Submission\n|-\n| style=\"width:60px;\" | {{center|(T)KO}}\n|  \n| (Technical) Knock Out\n|-\n|}\n{{Clear}}\n\n==The Ultimate Fighter China Finale: Kim vs. Hathaway==\n{{Infobox MMA event\n|name=The Ultimate Fighter China Finale: Kim vs. Hathaway\n|image=TUF China Finale.jpg\n|promotion=[[Ultimate Fighting Championship]]\n|date=March 1, 2014\n|venue=[[CotaiArena]]\n|city=[[Macau]], [[Special administrative regions of China|SAR]], China\n|attendance=6,000{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2014/03/tuf-china-finale-draws-announced-attendance-of-6000-in-macau/|title=TUF China Finale draws announced attendance of 6,000 in Macau|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|access-date=2014-03-01|date=2014-03-01}}\n|previousevent = [[UFC 170|UFC 170: Rousey vs. McMann]]\n|followingevent = [[UFC Fight Night: Gustafsson vs. Manuwa]]\n}}\n\n'''''The Ultimate Fighter China Finale: Kim vs. Hathaway''''' (also known as '''''UFC Fight Night: Kim vs. Hathaway''''') was a [[mixed martial arts]] event held by the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]]. The event took place on March 1, 2014, at the [[CotaiArena]] in [[Macau]], [[Special administrative regions of China|SAR]], China.\n\n===Background===\nThe event was headlined by a welterweight bout between [[John Hathaway (English wrestler)|John Hathaway]] and [[Dong Hyun Kim]].{{cite web|url=http://www.mmafighting.com/2014/1/3/5269444/john-hathaway-vs-dong-hyun-kim-to-headline-tuf-china-finale-in-march|title=John Hathaway vs. Dong Hyun Kim to headline TUF: China Finale in March|publisher=mmafighting.com|author=Ariel Helwani |date=2014-01-03|access-date=2013-01-03}}\n\nAlso featured on the card was the welterweight final of The Ultimate Fighter: China. The featherweight final between [[Ning Guangyou]] and Jianping Yang was removed from the card due to an injury to Jianping Yang.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2014/02/tourney-final-postponed-lee-vs-phan-promoted-to-tuf-china-finale-main-card/|title=Tourney final postponed, Lee vs. Phan promoted to TUF China Finale main card|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=MMAjunkie Staff |date=2014-02-23|access-date=2013-02-23}} The bout was rebooked on the [[UFC Fight Night: Bisping vs. Le]] card in August 2014.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2014/06/yang-jianping-vs-ning-guangyou-tourney-final-set-for-ufc-fight-night-48|title=Yang Jianping vs. Ning Guangyou tourney final set for UFC Fight Night 48|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|access-date=2014-06-25|date=2014-06-25}}\n\n[[Zak Cummings]] was expected to fight [[Alberto Mina]] but weighed in 8 pounds over. Mina would not agree to compete at a catch weight, so the bout was scratched from the event.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2014/02/zak-cummings-vs-alberto-mina-pulled-from-saturdays-tuf-china-finale/|title=Zak Cummings vs. Alberto Mina pulled from Saturday's TUF China Finale|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=MMAjunkie Staff |date=2014-02-28|access-date=2013-02-28}} With the removal of Cummings/Mina from the card, the event proceeded with only eight bouts, which is the smallest for the organization since [[UFC 72]] in 2007.{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2014/02/tuf-china-finales-new-eight-bout-lineup-set-gets-new-start-time/|title=TUF China Finale's new eight-bout lineup set|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=MMAjunkie Staff|date=2014-02-28|access-date=2013-02-28}}\n\n===Results===\n{{MMAevent}}\n{{MMAevent card|Main Card (UFC Fight Pass)}}\n{{MMAevent [[UFC 169|UFC 169: Barao vs. Faber 2]]\n|followingevent= [[UFC 170|UFC 170: Rousey vs. McMann]]\n}}\n\n'''''UFC Fight Night: Machida vs. Mousasi''''' (also known as '''''UFC Fight Night 36''''') was a [[mixed martial arts]] event held on February 15, 2014, at the [[Arena Jaraguá]] in [[Jaraguá do Sul]], [[Brazil]].{{cite web|url=https://sports.yahoo.com/news/lyoto-machida-vs-gegard-mousasi-225519731--mma.html|title=Lyoto Machida vs. Gegard Mousasi Expected to Headline UFC Fight Card in Brazil|publisher=sports.yahoo.com|author=Staff|date=2013-11-01|access-date=2013-11-01}} The event was broadcast live on [[Fox Sports 1]].{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2013/11/19/ufc-fight-night-36-co-main-event-set-jacare-souza-faces-off-with-francis-carmont-on-new-date/|title=UFC Fight Night 36 co-main event set: 'Jacare' Souza faces off with Francis Carmont on new date|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|date=2013-11-19|access-date=2013-11-19|archive-date=2015-09-05|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20150905183606/http://mmajunkie.com/2013/11/19/ufc-fight-night-36-co-main-event-set-jacare-souza-faces-off-with-francis-carmont-on-new-date/|url-status=dead}}\n\n==Background==\nThe event was headlined by a middleweight bout between former world champions [[Lyoto Machida]] and [[Gegard Mousasi]].\n\nReturning veteran [[Nate Loughran]] was expected to face [[Erick Silva]] at the event. However, Loughran was forced out of the bout with an injury{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2014/01/nate-loughran-out-at-ufc-fight-night-36-promotion-seeks-new-opponent-for-erick-silva/|title=Nate Loughran out at UFC Fight Night 36, promotion seeks new opponent for Erick Silva|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=John Morgan|date=2014-01-04|access-date=2013-01-04|archive-date=2014-01-08|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20140108051147/http://mmajunkie.com/2014/01/nate-loughran-out-at-ufc-fight-night-36-promotion-seeks-new-opponent-for-erick-silva/|url-status=dead}} and was replaced by promotional newcomer [[Takenori Sato]].{{cite web|url=https://sportv.globo.com/site/combate/noticia/2014/01/campeao-do-pancrase-e-contratado-pelo-ufc-e-estreia-contra-erick-silva.html|title=Campeão do Pancrase é contratado pelo UFC e estreia contra Erick Silva (Brazilian Portuguese)|publisher=sportv.globo.com|author=Ivan Raupp|date=2013-12-11|access-date=2013-12-11}}\n\n[[Zubaira Tukhugov]] was expected to face [[Thiago Tavares]] at this event. However, Tavares was forced to pull out due to an undisclosed injury, and was replaced by UFC newcomer [[Douglas Silva de Andrade]].{{cite web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2014/02/newcomer-douglas-silva-de-andrade-meets-zubair-tuhugov-at-ufc-fight-night-36/|title=Newcomer Douglas de Andrade meets Zubair Tuhugov at UFC Fight Night 36|publisher=mmajunkie.com|author=Staff|access-date=2014-02-06|date=2014-02-06|archive-date=2014-02-06|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20140206212208/http://mmajunkie.com/2014/02/newcomer-douglas-silva-de-andrade-meets-zubair-tuhugov-at-ufc-fight-night-36/|url-status=dead}}\n\nThis event was the first one to feature the new post-fight bonus structure. ''Fight of the Night'' bonuses remained, while the ''Submission of the Night'' and ''Knockout of the Night'' were both replaced by two ''Performance of the Night'' bonuses. They were awarded to fighters who display \"the two best individual performances on the card,\" according to a statement released by the UFC.{{cite web|url=https://www.mmafighting.com/2014/2/11/5402762/ufc-alters-post-fight-bonus-structure|title=UFC alters post-fight bonus structure |publisher=mmafighting.com|author=Shaun Al-Shatti|access-date=2014-02-11|date=2014-02-11}}\n\n==Results==\n{{MMAevent}}\n{{MMAevent card|Main card (Fox Sports 1)}}\n{{MMAevent bout\n|Middleweight\n|[[Lyoto Machida]]\n|def.\n|[[Gegard web|url=http://mmajunkie.com/2014/02/ufc-fight-night-36-bonuses-silva-oliveira-earn-first-performance-bonuses/|title=UFC Fight Night 36 bonuses: Silva, Oliveira earn first 'performance' '''Fight of the Night: Lyoto Machida vs. Gegard Mousasi'''\n* '''Performance of the Night: Erick Silva''' and '''Charles Oliveira'''\n\n==Records set==\nThe event equaled the record of most decisions on a UFC card set at [[UFC 169]], with ten decisions, but was later overtaken by [[UFC 263]] which had [[List_of_UFC_records#Most_Decision_bouts_in_one_event|eleven decisions]] in June 2021. The event also had a total fight time of 2:53:32, setting a new record for the longest event in UFC history, beating the previous record of 2:51:14, also set at UFC 169.{{cite web |url=https://www.sherdog.com/news/articles/By-the-Numbers-UFC-Fight-Night-36-63739|title=By the Numbers: UFC Fight Night 36|publisher=Sherdog|author=Tristen Critchfield|date=2014-02-16|access-date=2014-02-16}} \n\nThe records were later broken by [[UFC 263]] which had fight time of 3 hours, 19 minutes and 32 seconds and [[List_of_UFC_records#Most_Decision_bouts_in_one_event|eleven bouts in a UFC event]].{{cite web|url=https://mmajunkie.usatoday.com/lists/ufc-263-post-event-facts-new-record-most-fight-time-single-event-company-history|title=UFC 263 post-event facts: Decision-heavy card shatters record for most fight time|publisher=mmajunkie.usatoday.com|author=Mike Bohn|date=2021-06-13|access-date=2021-06-13}}\n\n==See also==\n*[[List of UFC events]]\n*[[2014 in America]]"}}}],"langlinks":[{"lang":"ja","title":"UFC Fight Night: Machida vs. Mousasi"}]},{"pageid":41251289,"ns":0,"title":"The Ultimate Fighter Nations: Canada vs. Australia","revisions":[{"slots":{"main":{"contentmodel":"wikitext","contentformat":"text/x-wiki","content":"{{short description|UFC mixed martial arts television series and event in 2014}}\n{{Infobox television\n| image = \n| image_size = \n| caption =\n|genre = [[reality television|Reality]], [[Broadcasting of sports events|Sports]]\n| runtime = 60 minutes\n| composer =\n| creator = [[Frank Fertitta III]], [[Lorenzo Fertitta]], [[Dana White]]\n| executive_producer = \n| starring = Dana White, [[Patrick Côté (fighter)|Patrick Côté]], [[Kyle Noke]]\n| country = [[Canada]]\n| network = [[FX Australia]], [[Fox Sports 1]], [[Sportsnet 360]]\n| company = \n| first_aired = {{Start date|2014|01|15}}\n| last_aired = {{End date|2014|04|09}}\n| num_episodes = \n}}\n\t\n'''''The Ultimate Fighter Nations: Canada vs. Australia''''' is an installment of the [[Ultimate Fighting Championship]] (UFC)-produced [[reality television]] series ''[[The Ultimate Fighter]]''. It is the fifth series produced outside the United States and the first filmed in Canada.\n\nThe series was officially announced by the UFC in June 2013.{{cite web |url=http://www.mmafighting.com/2013/6/14/4430122/patrick-cote-kyle-noke-selected-to-coach-the-ultimate-fighter-nations |title=Patrick Cote, Kyle Noke selected to coach 'The Ultimate Fighter Nations' |publisher=MMAJunkie.com |date=June 14, 2013 |access-date=December 2, 2013}} The coaches were announced as [[Patrick Côté (fighter)|Patrick Côté]] representing [[Canada]] and [[Kyle Noke]] representing [[Australia]]. This marked the second time, following [[The Ultimate Fighter: The Smashes]], that two former contestants from [[The Ultimate Fighter]] show are both coaches. Tryouts were held in September 2013 in [[Sydney]] and [[Toronto]].{{cite web |url=http://www.torontosun.com/2013/09/25/patrick-cote-pumped-to-lead-team-canada-in-the-ultimate-fighter |title=Patrick Cote pumped to lead Team Canada in The Ultimate Fighter |publisher=Torontosun.com |date=September 25, 2013 |access-date=December 2, 2013}} Fighters were chosen in the [[middleweight (MMA)|middleweight]] and [[welterweight (MMA)|welterweight]] divisions.{{cite web |url=http://mmajunkie.com/2013/11/tuf-nations-to-air-wednesdays-on-fox-sports-1-debuts-jan-15/ |title='TUF: Nations' to air Wednesdays on FOX Sports 1, debuts Jan. 15|publisher=MMAJunkie.com |date=November 30, 2013 |access-date=November 16, 2013}} The 16 fighters each competed in a single-elimination tournament, with the two division winners crowned in finale bouts that took place on April 16, 2014.\n\nThe show debuted on January 15, 2014. This season is the second to air on [[FX Australia]]. It also aired in the [[United States]] on [[Fox Sports 1]].\n\nThe TUF Nations Finale was held in Quebec City, Quebec on April 16, 2014.{{cite web |url=http://topmmanews.com/2013/12/10/ufc-headed-to-quebec-city-for-april-16-event/ |title=UFC Headed To Quebec City For April 16 Event |publisher=TopMMANews.com |date=December 10, 2013 |access-date=December 10, 2013}}\n\n==Cast==\n\n===Coaches===\n{{Col-begin}}\n{{Col-break}}\n;{{flagicon|CAN}} '''Team Canada'''\n*[[Patrick Côté (fighter)|Patrick Côté]], Head Coach\n*Kru Ash, Striking Coach\n*Fábio Holanda, Jiu Jitsu Coach\n*[[David Zilberman (wrestler)|David Zilberman]], Wrestling Coach\n{{Col-break}}\n;{{flagicon|AUS}} '''Team Australia'''\n*[[Kyle Noke]], Head Coach\n*[[Izzy Martinez|Israel Martinez]], Wrestling Coach\n*Adrian Pang, Striking Coach\n*Roberto Tussa, Jiu Jitsu Coach\n{{col-end}}\n\n===Fighters===\n*'''Team Canada'''\n**'''Welterweights:''' [[Olivier Aubin-Mercier]], Matthew Desroches, [[Kajan Johnson]], and [[Chad Laprise]].\n**'''Middleweights:''' Luke Harris, [[Nordine Taleb]], [[Elias Theodorou]], and [[Sheldon Westcott]].\n*'''Team Australia'''\n**'''Welterweights:''' Chris Indich, [[Jake Matthews (fighter)|Jake Matthews]], [[Brendan O'Reilly (fighter)|Brendan O’Reilly]], and [[Richard Walsh (MMA)|Richard Walsh.]]\n**'''Middleweights:''' Vik Grujic, [[Daniel Kelly (judoka)|Daniel Kelly]], Tyler Manawaroa, and Zein Saliba.\n\n==Episodes==\n\n;'''Episode 1 – Strangers in a Strange Land: (January 15, 2014)'''\n*The fighters enter the Ultimate Fighter training centre in Quebec City, Quebec, Canada and shortly after both coaches arrive. Côté has the coin to decide the first fight pick with him but after he says that he feels pretty confident looking at his team, he hands it over to Noke. He flips the coin and Team Canada wins it. Côté reminds that they have the first fight pick and will announce it in a couple of days. He also reminds everyone to enjoy this \"once in a lifetime opportunity\".\n*During a cutscene Dana White announces that fighters could receive $25,000 for ''Knockout of the Season'', ''Submission of the Season'' and ''Fight of the Season''. Also there's a $5,000 bonus for finishes in every fight.\n*Both coaches share some of their experience as former contenders on ''The Ultimate Fighter'' to their respective teams.\n*The fighters meet the house and it has a distinctly Canadian feeling, as the structure is made almost entirely of wood and is located in a remote area of Quebec City.\n*The Australian fighters are having a hard time with the long travel and different time zone from their home.\n*The fighters meet White for the first time during a video conference via [[Xbox One]]. The first fight pick is announced by Côté and it is Team Canada's Kajan Johnson vs. Team Australia's Brendan O’Reilly. Johnson wanted to fight first even before he met Côté.\n*During the night, the Canadian fighters discuss about their opponents records and experience but the Australians could hear everything from their bedrooms and O'Reilly confronts them about what they just said.\n*Johnson mentions a couple of injuries he has and states that he always fight injured but even in that situation he states that he is more dangerous than anybody else there.\n*'''Kajan Johnson''' defeated Brendan O’Reilly via submission (rear-naked choke) in round 1.\n*Côté chooses the next match-up: Team Canada's Elias Theodorou vs. Team Australia's Zein Saliba.\n\n;'''Episode 2 – Bad Hair Day: (January 22, 2014)'''\n*The Canadians have a good time at the house and they mention that Johnson's victory helped bring them close together.\n*Taleb decides to call a private meeting for team Canada after he sees some of his teammates telling information about their own skills and records to the Australians.\n*MMA fighter and four-time [[Brazilian jiu-jitsu]] world champion [[Vítor Ribeiro]] is invited as a guest coach for team Canada and does a special session with the fighters.\n*Salibas mentions that he knows nothing about his opponent and Theodorou takes a moment to talk about his career as a model and actor besides fighting.\n*Team Canada holds a dinner for Côté and the other coaches, including Côté's nutritionist Jean-François Gaudreau. They all have a conversation about how important nutrition is to an elite fighter.\n*It's Kelly's birthday and his coaches bring him a cake to celebrate it. After Noke pranks Kelly and food fight starts after that between the whole team.\n*'''Elias Theodorou''' defeated Zein Saliba via unanimous decision in two rounds.\n*Côté chooses the next match-up: Team Canada's Chad Laprise vs. Team Australia's Chris Indich.\n\n;'''Episode 3 – The Bible Thumper: (January 29, 2014)'''\n*Early in the morning Manawaroa uses a [[didgeridoo]] to annoy the Canadians and he's successful, especially with Taleb.\n*Strength and conditioning coach Jonathan Chaimberg, who is Canadian, shows up to help Team Australia. He used to train with Laprise and Taleb from the other team and he's there to help Noke. Côté mentions that Chaimberg is \"so full of himself\" that he might not even care about his ties to other fighters in the opposite team. As Chaimberg thought, most of the fighters struggle post-training with the intense pace. After the training ends, Team Canada arrives for their training session and meet with Chaimberg. Some of them later discuss their opinions on the subject.\n*Indich talks about his relationship with his dad in the past and how it affected him. Meanwhile, Laprise claims this will be the first time in his career where he's been completely injury-free. He talks about his road in the sport up to TUF and how his strong religious beliefs serve as motivation to succeed.\n*Manawaroa has some problems with an injured finger and his weight. The coaches are concerned about it and try to make him motivated to train hard and also make weight.\n*Indich says he feels the pressure to not only get the win for himself, but to get the first victory for his team and change the mood around them.\n*'''Chad Laprise''' defeated Chris Indich via unanimous decision in two rounds. Indich's performance was praised by everyone after their fight.\n*Côté chooses the next match-up: Team Canada's Nordine Taleb vs. Team Australia's Tyler Manawaroa.\n\n;'''Episode 4: (February 5, 2014)'''\n*Following last episode's fight, Indich and Taleb have a discussion in front of the Australian team regarding comments the Canadians said about Indich not being able to take a punch and that this series would be the first one to feature a team winning 8–0 in quarterfinal fights. Later in the house is visible that the Laprise-Indich fight brought both teams closer in terms of respect, specially towards Indich.\n*Taleb and his teammates all believe that regardless of Manawaroa's record, Taleb's previous opponents were better than his current foe and they all believe he has the better skills. Meanwhile, the Australians believe that their teammate will give them the first win and Manawaroa is ready for it.\n*Both fighters have an intense stare down after the weigh-in.\n*'''Tyler Manawaroa''' defeated Nordine Taleb via unanimous decision in three rounds.\n*In a hospital waiting for exams, Taleb and Manawaroa debate their strategies for the fight and they agree that this fight will probably be the fight of the season.\n*Noke chooses the next match-up: Team Australia's Richard Walsh vs. Team Canada's Matthew Desroches.\n\n;'''Episode 5: (February 12, 2014)'''\n*The fighters are talking about the shift in momentum in the competition after Team Australia finally earned a victory.\n*Taleb and Manawaroa decide to blow off some steam together at the house in the form of a drinking competition.\n*At the next Team Canada training session, Côté tries to boost his team's morale after suffering its first loss of the season. Deroches receives personal attention from the coaching staff as he readies for his upcoming fight.\n*Walsh stresses the importance of building off Manawaroa's victory and helping his team get back in the competition.\n*During training, Noke and Martinez discuss about Kelly's resilience during training considering that he has injuries on his ankles and feet.\n*Despite a broken foot, Taleb remains on the team and helps his teammates with training and advising. He became an unofficial \"fifth coach\". Later Westcott injuries his knee during a sparring session against Theodorou and Team Canada is worried that it might affect his usual gameplan. They assure that they need to win the next fight to make sure they have the last middleweight fight picks.\n*'''Richard Walsh''' defeated Matthew Desroches via unanimous decision in two rounds.\n*Noke chooses the next match-up: Team Australia's Daniel Kelly vs. Team Canada's Sheldon Westcott.\n\n;'''Episode 6: (February 19, 2014)'''\n*After Walsh's victory, Kelly explains that the Canadians seem nicer now that they've suffered defeats.\n*Chad Laprise expresses desire to fight Richard Walsh, a fight that Kajan Johnson wants as well.\n*During training, Côté is absent and his assistant coaches take over the session.\n*Jake Matthews suffers through a stomach ache during training, but assistant coach Israel Martinez shows no remorse and gives Matthews a hard time.\n*At the house, the fighters are treated to snacks and beer with a viewing of [[UFC 167]].\n*Daniel Kelly reveals that his son is suffering from a rare diseases called [[cystinosis]] and that a kidney transplant, along with being financially stable is something Kelly wants to produce for his family.\n*'''Sheldon Westcott''' defeated Daniel Kelly via submission (arm triangle choke) in round 1.\n*It was revealed that Kelly had injured his right knee during the fight and is taken off to the hospital for treatment.\n*Côté announces the final preliminary welterweight matchup: Team Canada's Olivier Aubin-Mercier vs Team Australia's Jake Matthews.\n\n;'''Episode 7: '''\n*'''Olivier Aubin-Mercier''' defeated Jake Matthews via unanimous decision after 2 rounds.\n\n;'''Episode 8:'''\n*'''Vik Grujic''' defeated Luke Harris via KO (elbows) in round 1.\n\n;'''Episode 9:'''\n*recap episode\n\n;'''Episode 10:'''\n*'''Chad Laprise''' defeated Kajan Johnson via KO (punch) in round 2.\n\n;'''Episode 11:'''\n*'''Elias Theodorou''' defeated Tyler Manawaroa via unanimous decision after 3 rounds.\n\n;'''Episode 12:'''\n*'''Olivier Aubin-Mercier''' defeated Richard Walsh via submission (rear naked choke) in round 1.\n\n;'''Episode 13:'''\n*'''Sheldon Westcott''' defeated Vik Grujic via submission (side choke) in round 1.\n\n==Tournament bracket==\n\n===Welterweight bracket===\n{{Round8\n\n\n||{{flagicon|CAN}}
